### Page 5

Price objective basis & risk 
NYPCB (NANYF) 
Our PO of NT$2,000 is based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E. We view this multiple as fair 
given ROE and operating margin expansion to 39% and 41% in 2027-28E (vs 13% and 
14% in 2025-26E).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) higher SiP/AiP adoption, and (3) 
less intensified competition pressure in PCB business.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) muted SiP/AiP adoption, 
and (3) more severe competition in PCB business.

The 25th edition of the EM Sentiment Survey

Less bull, more bite

◆ Smartphone companies have gone from being demand drivers 
to price takers 
◆ But we think if memory price inflation slows, that could be 
enough for margins to improve before shipments increase 
◆ In a separate report published today, we initiate coverage of 
Xiaomi with a Buy rating and a TP of HKD33.20 
Hope amid hard times. This report looks at what component shortages and higher 
memory prices mean for the economics of the smartphone market. The key message 
is that while average selling prices have jumped as costs surged, handset makers 
don’t need memory prices to fall to recover. Any slowdown in AI-driven memory price 
inflation could be enough for margins to improve before shipments start to increase.  
Stabilisation in 2027, recovery in 2028. IDC forecasts that global smartphone 
shipments will fall 16.7% y-o-y in 2026 – the steepest contraction in industry history – 
decline a further 2.9% in 2027, and rebound 4.5% y-o-y in 2028.  The low base in 2026 
should release some replacement demand but higher handset ASPs and a limited supply 
of low-end memory chips will likely limit the recovery. In China, handset inventories rose 
in 2H25 and 1Q26 before easing in 2Q, suggesting the shock of higher memory costs 
arrived before inventory and demand had normalised. We expect the first green shoots to 
appear in ASPs and gross margin, ahead of shipment numbers. 
Memory inflation hit margins before consumer prices. Spot pricing moves first, 
followed by contract prices, handset makers’ blended procurement costs, component 
inventory consumption, handset bill of materials (BOM) and reported gross margin. 
Lower-cost memory inventory delays the impact on earnings, while finished goods 
and channel inventories delay the ability to reprice. We see 4Q25-1Q26 as the 
margin-absorption phase, with the larger earnings impact likely to run through 2H26.  
Higher costs to be passed on through new products. The main levers for handset 
makers are higher prices for new models, fewer discounts, slower RAM and storage 
upgrades, wider premiums for high-memory models and savings elsewhere in the BOM. 
Times are particularly tough because AI/server demand is tightening memory supply at a 
time when smartphone demand remains weak. In previous cycles, smartphones and PCs 
were major sources of DRAM and NAND demand, so higher memory costs coincided 
with better end demand. This time, handset makers are having to accept supplier-driven 
price increases without the same support from sales volumes.  
MLCC risk. AI servers use more and higher-spec multi-layer ceramic capacitors 
(MLCCs), shifting supply to higher-value products. The effect on handset profits should be 
limited as these key components make up only a small part of the BOM. However, supply 
shortages can still disrupt production – if consumer-grade components are scarce, finding 
replacements takes time. See our Xiaomi initiation report.

Why we think the smartphone market won’t recover in 2027 
The decline in 2026 will not create a strong replacement cycle next year 
According to IDC forecasts made in June 2026, global smartphone shipments will fall 16.7%  
y-o-y in 2026e to just over 1bn units, decline a further 2.9% in 2027e, and then rebound 4.5% in 
2028e when memory supply begins to normalise.  
The low base should support replacement demand, but affordability remains the constraint. Higher 
memory costs are raising the bill of materials (BOM) for handsets and pushing manufacturers to 
either increase prices or reduce entry-level supply. The thinking is that a consumer who defers an 
upgrade in 2026 does not necessarily return in 2027 if the replacement device is materially more 
expensive, or offers less memory at the same price. We think premium markets should be more 
resilient, while entry-level Android demand is more exposed.

Exhibit 2. Global smartphone shipments: 
recovery pushed out to 2028e 
 Exhibit 3. China smartphone inventories* 
were still elevated when memory costs 
began to rise

Source: IDC estimates, HSBC  *Inventory proxy: China’s smartphone production + imports – China’s smartphone 
shipment – exports. 
Source: NBS, GAC, CAICT, HSBC

The first-half vendor data already show this split. Global shipments declined 5.5% y-o-y in 1H26. 
Apple and Samsung shipments rose 5.8% and 8.7%, respectively, while those for Xiaomi, 
OPPO and Vivo fell 22.8%, 13.8% and 13.5%, respectively (source: IDC). Memory is not the 
only explanation: product timing, geography, channel execution and inventory also matter. 
However, the divergence is consistent with stronger resilience at the premium end and greater 
volume pressure in price-sensitive Android segments.  
China’s implied handset-inventory series point in the same direction. Inventory rose through 
2H25 and reached another high in 1Q26 before easing in 2Q26. The series is calculated as 
production plus imports less domestic shipments and exports. It should be treated as a 
directional supply-chain proxy rather than actual channel inventory because the underlying 
datasets use different definitions and timing. The turning point is nevertheless useful: 
manufacturers entered the memory upcycle before the finished-goods cycle had fully normalised. 
Memory pressure extends beyond smartphones, but the earnings sensitivity varies 
materially by product 
TVs have seen a meaningful increase in memory’s share of BOM as prices rose, while 
wearables, audio devices and other IoT products generally carry lower absolute memory 
content and therefore less direct P&L sensitivity. In these categories, selected niche-memory 
availability and channel inventory & pricing can matter more than headline DRAM or NAND 
moves. We are beginning to see signs that pricing momentum is moderating in some consumer 
and niche categories, although conditions remain tight in higher-capacity products. Our sector

Higher memory prices do not directly determine smartphone 
shipments 
Why memory inflation is not the sole cause of the fall in smartphone demand 
Xiaomi’s historical shipment data suggests that the relationship is weak. Mobile DRAM prices 
have moved through several cycles since 2018, while Xiaomi’s quarterly shipments have been 
driven more visibly by product launches and seasonality. Falling memory prices have not 
consistently triggered stronger shipments, while rising prices have not resulted in an immediate 
or proportionate decline in units.

This does not mean memory prices are irrelevant with regards to demand. Consumers do not
replace a phone because DRAM or NAND becomes cheaper, and they do not automatically
cancel a replacement because memory becomes more expensive. Replacement age, macro
conditions, product innovation, subsidies, retail financing and channel inventory remain the main
drivers of unit sales. Memory prices affect demand indirectly once higher component costs

change retail prices, available specifications, or the supply of low-end models.
What is different this time is the source of the price increase. In earlier cycles, smartphones and
PCs were large contributors to incremental DRAM and NAND demand. The same healthy end
demand that tightened memory supply also helped handset makers raise ASP and improve mix.
In 2026, AI servers and high bandwidth memory (HBM) are pulling capacity and capital towards
higher-return applications, while consumer electronics demand remains weak.

TrendForce, an industry research company based in Taiwan, expects overall mobile DRAM price
hikes of around 8-13% q-o-q in 3Q26, given Samsung’s already elevated base in 2Q26. The supply
of low-power double data rate (LPDDR) RAM, a specialized type of computer memory designed to
use less power and take up less physical space than standard RAM, is still tight.
In terms of NAND, TrendForce expects price increases of 10-15% in 3Q26, with contract prices

already at record highs. As a result, we believe that weaker smartphone production may not be
enough to bring memory prices down quickly. AI/server demand can continue to absorb
available supply, leaving handset makers with less bargaining power than in a consumer
electronics-led cycle.
When are higher memory prices reflected in reported earnings?
The simple answer is later than suggested by the spot price chart below (Exhibit 8), and with no
fixed lag. Large smartphone OEMs procure most memory under contract rather than buying

directly in the spot market. Spot prices affect negotiations and indicate direction, but the
relevant cost is the OEM's blended contract price across suppliers, memory generations and
purchase periods.
The transmission path: Spot price → contract price → OEM procurement cost → component
inventory consumption → handset BOM → reported gross margin.
Rolling inventory delays the transmission in both directions. When contract prices rise, lower-
cost DRAM and NAND inventory can temporarily protect reported margin. When memory prices

stabilise or fall, more expensive inventory may still flow through the cost of goods sold (COGS)
before the savings become visible. Existing finished goods and channel inventory can delay the
impact further, as OEMs may need to sell older models before new launches can reset prices
and specifications.
Exhibit 8 is consistent with a lagged but not fixed transmission of memory costs. Mobile DRAM
prices began declining in 3Q22 and Xiaomi’s smartphone margin subsequently improved, while

the increase in mobile DRAM prices from 3Q24 was followed by weaker margins in 2025. This
sequence is consistent with inventory-led cost transmission, but it does not establish a structural
asymmetry or a fixed 2-3 quarters lag. Product mix, promotion, channel inventory and other
component costs also affect Xiaomi’s reported gross margin.
We believe 4Q25-1Q26 likely marked the initial absorption period, when contract price began
resetting but lower-cost inventory still provided some protection. The impact of higher

procurement costs should become more visible in 2H26 as inventories turn, although the timing
and magnitude would depend on Xiaomi’s procurement contracts, inventory position, shipment
mix and product launch schedule. Margins could improve in 2027e if memory prices stabilise
and new products provide greater scope for pricing, specs and mix adjustments.

MLCCs: smaller cost, but a potentially larger operational risk 
Multi-layer ceramic capacitors (MLCCs), the components that store and release electrical 
energy to stabilize voltage, are a key part of the story. AI demand is creating a more selective 
MLCC squeeze than the broad-based memory inflation discussed above. AI servers require 
substantially more MLCC content and a greater mix of high-capacitance and higher-
specification parts than conventional servers. This does not mean smartphones directly 
compete for every component: the AI-grade components where supply is tightest and 
mainstream handset MLCCs often have different specifications. 
Instead, the transmission mechanism is through capacity allocation and supply-chain behaviour. 
Japanese and Korean suppliers are increasingly prioritising higher-value AI and infrastructure 
products, while longer lead times, distributor restocking and precautionary ordering are 
beginning to spill over into some consumer-grade categories. Recent industry data point to a 
bifurcated market – tight high-end supply alongside a broader, but still uneven, tightening in 
mainstream products.  
For smartphone handset makers, the direct earnings exposure is much smaller than for memory 
because MLCC accounts for only a small share of BOM. The operational risk, however, can be 
disproportionate. MLCCs are low-cost but specification- and qualification-sensitive components; 
replacing an unavailable part may require supplier qualification or board-level validation. A 
shortage in a relatively inexpensive component can delay production even when its impact on 
BOM cost is immaterial. 
We view memory primarily as an earnings and affordability bottleneck, while MLCC represents a 
second-order production risk. The key factor to watch is whether today’s tight supply of high-end 
components broadens into sustained shortages of qualified consumer-grade parts. 
Premium brands have more room to pass through costs; mass-
market brands have more margin exposure 
Can Xiaomi pass through higher memory costs? 
We believe the company is unlikely to pass through the full increase without affecting demand. 
Xiaomi’s lower handset ASP leaves a smaller gross profit pool per device, while competition in 
mass-market price bands limits the scope for headline price increases. Xiaomi can partly offset 
the pressure through premiumisation, specification adjustments and lower promotional intensity, 
but it will likely absorb part of the higher cost through smartphone gross margin.

Our bottom-up estimates show that memory represents a meaningful and rising share of 
Xiaomi’s handset cost base (Exhibit 9). For the selected Xiaomi models in our sample, 
estimated memory cost was generally equivalent to approximately 6-16% of handset COGS 
before 2024. Under our mobile DRAM and NAND contract price assumptions, the ratio rises to 
approximately 35% for the model selected in 2026 (Mi 18 Pro). Selected iPhone models are 
included as a benchmark: the corresponding ratio reaches approximately 25% in 2026e, as the 
iPhone’s higher selling price provides a larger cost and gross profit buffer.

The comparison does not suggest that Xiaomi necessarily uses more memory per device. 
Rather, a similar dollar increase in DRAM and NAND cost has a larger proportional impact on a 
lower-priced model. Xiaomi could raise prices to protect unit economics, but this risks 
weakening demand in price-sensitive markets. It could also reduce memory specifications or 
promotional intensity, although these measures may affect product competitiveness. 
Premiumisation provides the most constructive offset, but is unlikely to fully neutralise the near-
term cost increase across Xiaomi’s portfolio. 
Pass-through likely to come through new product launches 
Increasing the official price of an existing handset is usually impractical because the product 
already has a retail reference point and channel inventory. The adjustment instead takes place 
at the next launch. 
◆ The first lever is the launch price and effective ASP. OEMs can raise the next 
generation's starting price, reduce launch subsidies, cut discounts or push demand toward 
Pro and Ultra variants. The nominal entry price can remain unchanged while realised ASP 
rises because the base model receives less promotion or has lower availability. 
◆ The second lever is memory specification. Upgrades from 8GB to 12GB of RAM or from 
256GB to 512GB of storage can be delayed. The base SKU can retain a smaller 
configuration while premium models continue to upgrade. TrendForce still expects average 
smartphone DRAM capacity to reach 8.5GB in 2026, up 10% y-o-y, but the average masks 
a larger split between premium and low-end models (source: TrendForce, May 2026). 
◆ The third lever is stock keeping unit (SKU) structure. OEMs can widen the price gap 
between 256GB and 512GB products, remove a low-priced high-memory version, or 
reserve newer memory technology for flagship models. This recovers more of the cost from 
less price-sensitive buyers. 
◆ The fourth lever is cost reduction elsewhere. OEMs can press camera, display, mechanical 
and other suppliers for savings, trim other specifications, reduce promotional spending or 
accept temporary margin pressure. Memory inflation can become a pricing-pressure event for 
the wider handset supply chain even if the official handset price changes only modestly.

### Page 8

Equities ● Electronic Equipment & Instruments 
30 September 2026 
8 
2027: margins to recover before unit sales 
By 2027, most OEMs should have completed one or two launch cycles under the higher 
memory-cost regime. This should allow higher effective ASP, narrower discounts, redesigned 
memory tiers and lower low-end exposure to stabilise gross margin. IDC's forecast of a further 
2.9% shipment decline is therefore compatible with better earnings. 
For Xiaomi, the key question is whether new launches restore smartphone gross margin without 
causing a disproportionate loss of unit shipments. Our base case is for a slight decrease in  
shipments in 2027e, while ASP continues to improve and gross margin stabilises. The bull case 
would require memory supply to normalise earlier, allowing OEMs to restore lower-priced 
products while deferred replacement demand is released. The bear case is persistent LPDDR 
tightness combined with weaker consumer demand, forcing another round of production cuts, 
specification reductions and margin absorption. 
The bottom line 
Global smartphone shipments are unlikely to recover meaningfully in 2027, but OEM earnings 
could improve as new product cycles enable greater cost pass-through and margin 
normalisation. For Xiaomi, the reported earnings recovery will depend on inventory turnover and 
execution on pricing and mix, while the equity market may anticipate the inflection before it 
becomes visible in reported margins.

### Page 1

Taiwan Industrials

Investment case: We initiate coverage of Acter, an established 
multinational cleanroom engineering, procurement, construction and 
management (EPCM) turnkey service provider, with a Buy (1) rating. Its 
core business includes cleanroom electromechanical engineering, 
water/gas/chemical delivery systems, and other cleanroom services. We 
believe now is a good time to revisit the stock as it underperformed the 
TAIEX Index by 32.7% in 3Q26, mainly on weaker-than-expected 2Q26 
results and soft sector sentiment. Given its stronger revenue momentum 
over recent months and upcoming TSMC results on 15 October, which may 
drive sentiment, we suggest investors to buy the stock. We forecast a 
24.6% revenue CAGR and 36.9% net profit CAGR over 2026-28E.

Intact global demand for cleanroom technology, the edge AI market, 
and rapid cleanroom expansion in Asia further support Acter’s order 
backlog. Its backlog reached TWD66bn in July 2026 vs. TWD46bn in 2025 
and TWD30bn in 2022 (15.3% 2022-25 CAGR), which largely outpaced the 
global cleanroom technology market’s forecast CAGR over 2023-2032E, 
according to Precedence Research.

Strong competitive edge supports order share gains across multiple 
segments. Acter’s competitive edge lies in 1) intelligent management 
enabled by digital twins and AI, using 3D building information modelling 
(BIM) and AI-driven digital twin management; 2) intellectualisation of green 
engineering, which comprises green design, green procurement, and green 
techniques; 3) experience across multiple industries (ie, data centres and 
electromechanical engineering for the consumer industry) which supports 
better integration; and 4) a complete network, enabling on-the-spot support. 
Acter believes it has the highest market share among the operators of three 
major US data centre players due largely to its capabilities in liquid cooling 
and its expertise in electromechanical and cleanroom engineering.

Catalysts: Near-term: project wins from key clients, supporting stronger 
monthly revenue momentum; mid- to long-term: an improving operating-
margin trend and a resilient order backlog.

Valuation: We are the first non-domestic broker to cover Acter. We initiate 
coverage with a 12-month TP of TWD1,415, based on a target PER of 20x 
(equivalent to a 0.54x PEG over 2026-28E). We believe our valuation is 
fair, given Acter’s strong earnings visibility, which we believe warrants a 
rerating. Our TP is supported by a DCF valuation of TWD1,454.

Risks: 1) weaker-than-expected expansion in the semiconductor industry; 
and 2) project delays or cancellations.

7 October 2026

Acter

Initiation: acting on the demand for cleanrooms

➢ Key beneficiary of the cleanroom shortage in the high-tech industry 
➢ Fast-growing order backlog likely to be realised over 2026-28E 
➢ Initiating with a Buy (1) rating and a 12-month TP of TWD1,415

### Page 2

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026

How do we justify our view? 
Growth outlook Valuation Earnings revisions

Growth outlook

Acter: net profit and net-profit growth    
We forecast Acter to record a 36.9% net profit CAGR over 
2026-28E, on the back of a 24.6% revenue CAGR over the 
same period, driven by 1) larger project scale, as strong 
demand for cleanroom EPCM services from the high-tech 
sector is likely to support continued growth in Acter’s order 
backlog and 2) improving gross margins and operating 
efficiency. We expect operating margin to expand by 2.0pp 
in 2027E and 1.3pp in 2028E, driven by favourable gross 
margins on new projects and operating leverage.

Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts

Valuation   Acter: 1-year-forward PER bands    
Our 12-month TP of TWD1,415 is based on a target PER 
of 20x, applied to our 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Our 
target multiple of 20x is higher than the stock’s past-3-year 
1-year-forward PER (12.2x) and past-5-year 1-year-
forward PER (9.6x) and implies a PEG of 0.54x on our 
36.9% net profit CAGR forecast over 2026-28E. We 
believe the stock deserves a rerating in the next 12 
months, largely supported by resilient cleanroom demand 
globally.

Our TP is also supported by an alternative DCF-derived 
valuation of TWD1,454/share.

Source: Bloomberg, Daiwa forecasts

Earnings revisions

Acter: Bloomberg consensus EPS forecast revisions   
Bloomberg consensus EPS estimates for 2026E and 
2027E have risen 24.8% and 24.3% YTD, respectively. 
This is likely due to upward revisions to order backlog and 
earnings forecasts following better-than-expected industry 
outlook. We are the first foreign broker to cover Acter.

Source: Bloomberg

Company profile 
Established in 1979, Acter Group (Acter) is a leading engineering services provider specialising in 
cleanroom, electromechanical, and high-tech facility integration solutions for semiconductor, 
optoelectronics, biotech, green energy, and advanced manufacturing clients across Taiwan, China and 
the rest of the world. As of 2025, its revenue comprised cleanroom electromechanical (71%), water, gas 
and chemical delivery systems (20%), consumer industry electromechanical (4%), biomedical (2%) and 
high-tech equipment & materials sales & services (3%).

### Page 5

5

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Acting on the demand for cleanrooms 
Why are cleanrooms a bottleneck in the high-tech 
industry? 
In recent years, cleanrooms have become a critical bottleneck for high-tech industries, 
especially semiconductor manufacturing, OSAT, AI hardware, and PCB, mainly because of 
its indispensable nature, severe supply-demand imbalance, labour shortages, and high 
technical and regulatory barriers.

Cleanrooms are an indispensable core infrastructure as they determine product 
yield and quality stability. Advanced manufacturing nodes in semiconductors, 
optoelectronics, and biopharmaceuticals require highly particle-free, constant-temperature, 
and constant-humidity environments to ensure reliable production. As process 
technologies continue to advance, such as 2nm node (or even newer nodes) and 
advanced packaging, existing cleanrooms must be rebuilt, and the complexity of building 
new ones thus increases. This keeps overall demand for cleanrooms at high levels.

A severe global supply-demand imbalance with demand for cleanroom construction 
outpacing supply. The surge in AI servers, high-performance computing (HPC), and high-
bandwidth memory (HBM) has triggered a global expansion for front-end wafer fabs, 
packaging and testing facilities, and PCB manufacturing. Order visibility remains extremely 
strong across the industry. According to Markets and Markets, the global cleanroom market 
size is set to expand at a CAGR of 6.6% over 2025-31E. At the same time, the global 
semiconductor cleanroom market size is set to grow at a CAGR of 8.0% over 2025-30E. 
For instance, Acter’s order backlog exceeded TWD66bn at the end of July 2026, up from 
TWD46bn at the end of 2025, showing the urgency of demand for cleanroom construction.

Global: cleanroom market size and growth      Global: semiconductor cleanroom market size and growth

Source: Markets and Markets  Source: Markets and Markets

Human resources are an additional limitation to capacity growth. Cleanroom 
engineering is highly technical and labour-intensive, relying on specialised on-site 
installation and supervision that cannot be replaced by robots. At the same time, the 
industry faces severe labour shortages and an aging workforce, and younger generations 
are reluctant to join field operations. Cross-border staff deployment is also less cost-
efficient than local hiring.

Cleanrooms involve complex system integration, creating high barriers to entry. A 
cleanroom construction includes 4 sequential phases, where each phase usually requires 
totally different areas of expertise. Therefore, the competitive landscape in each area 
differs. The process begins with civil & structural (C&S) works to establish the physical 
plant, followed by mechanical, electrical, and plumbing (MEP) works for essential utility 
generation, where Acter is one of the leading global experts. After MEP is finished, 
chemicals/gas/water are then distributed via main systems through primary piping 
network and finally delivered to production equipment through hook-up systems. As

### Page 6

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
global supply chains restructure under N+1 (domestic market + 1) strategies, companies 
building overseas facilities in places like the US face strict and complicated licensing 
processes, particularly on fire safety and hazardous chemicals. Without experience dealing 
with such engineering and safety issues, construction timelines can be severely delayed.

As a result, even with advanced equipment and chip designs, a high-tech company cannot 
begin production without adequate cleanroom space, skilled engineers, and system-
integration capabilities. Therefore, cleanroom engineering contractors hold significant 
bargaining power and capacity allocation leverage in today's tech supply chain.

IC foundry 
In the foundry sector, where the cleanroom supply-demand shortage is the tightest, project 
activity remains centred on advanced process capacity expansion in Taiwan and Arizona. 
Simultaneously, domestic advanced node development remains intense, with multi-phase 
fab builds advancing across Hsinchu, Taichung, and Kaohsiung. Local cleanroom and MEP 
integration demand is further boosted by advanced packaging facilities in Taiwan (such as 
Chiayi and Tainan) and upcoming projects in the US are set to contribute revenue for the 
cleanroom providers in the following years.

Memory 
The memory segment is exhibiting robust momentum as well, mainly catalysed by HBM 
capacity additions. Projects such as the plant in Japan, transitioned to contribution for the 
cleanroom EPCM providers starting from 2026. In Taiwan, turnkey engineering contracts in 
Miaoli are also actively underway. This trend is reinforced by stronger-than-expected 
expansion activity from memory producers in China.

OSAT,  PCB and ODM/OEM 
For OSAT companies, demand is also surging due to the spill-over effect of advanced 
packaging protocols and N+1 supply chain restructuring. The OSAT names are expanding 
their footprint both in Southern Taiwan and in overseas locations including in the US and 
Southeast Asia. Major engineering contracts signed between OSAT clients and Acter show 
stable long-term workload for strong cleanroom demand over our forecast horizon. 
Similarly, overseas expansion for PCB manufacturing is peaking in Southeast Asia, 
especially in Thailand. We expect Chinese PCB players to also increase their capacity, just 
like memory companies in the past year. Except for front-end and back-end chip 
manufacturing, ODM/OEM and data centre infrastructure is also a key growth engine for 
the EPCM industry. Major Taiwanese ODMs are building production facilities in Texas and 
Mexico to meet local manufacturing needs. Cleanroom contractors, such as Acter, 
providing MEP and system integration in Texas are directly benefiting from these projects. 
The shift toward liquid cooling systems has also increased engineering complexity, 
favouring few cleanroom companies with the expertise to deliver complex integration for 
CSPs in both North America and Southeast Asia.

Acter: an integrated cleanroom solutions provider  
Acter Group (Acter), an integrated cleanroom solutions provider, maintains its competitive 
positioning within the high-tech cleanroom and MEP engineering sector, driven by its group 
synergy and global footprint. Acter operates as a comprehensive vendor through a group 
business model. The parent company, Acter, supervises overall cleanroom and MEP 
services, while subsidiary Nova Technology (Novatech, 6613 TT, not rated) mainly provides 
chemical primary system and hook-up, and Novatech’s subsidiary Rayzher (7703 TT, not 
rated) provides gas primary system, hook-up and equipment. In China, Acter operates 
through Acter Technology Integration Group (603163 CH, not rated) and Suzhou Winmax 
(not listed) for Chinese EPCM (engineering, procurement, construction, and management) 
and equipment business. Except for C&S (civil and structural) works, where the margin is 
low (7-8%) and the contract duration is long, Acter’s services span nearly the entire 
cleanroom construction process. Its one-stop solutions model supports customer retention 
Acter provides 
integrated cleanroom 
solutions via various 
subsidiaries

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
and improves bid-win rates, particularly for overseas expansion, where customers have 
few reliable providers to choose from.

Acter: organisational chart

Source: Company, Daiwa

To avoid the risk of over-concentration, Acter diversifies its client portfolio across 
semiconductor foundries, memory, PCB, OSAT, ODM hardware assembly, CSP data 
centres and the consumer industry. By sector, semiconductor projects represent the largest 
share of Acter’s portfolio, accounting for 66% of total revenue in 1H26, vs. 69% in 2025. 
Foundry manufacturing is the largest source of demand for cleanrooms in Taiwan, 
especially as advanced-node capacity expands at sites in Hsinchu, Taichung and 
Kaohsiung. In the memory segment, the company executes turnkey engineering builds to 
expand its capacity aggressively, creating cleanroom opportunities across Taiwan, Japan 
and Singapore. This multi-sector coverage decreases the risk of cyclical downturns in any 
individual segment, in our view.

By establishing 33 operating entities across 11 international regions, the company 
effectively captures capex migration driven by global N+1 supply chain diversification. This 
allows Acter to support Taiwanese and international clients as they expand manufacturing 
capacity from Taiwan into Southeast Asia, Japan and the US. Due to the complexity of 
advanced-node IC manufacturing, packaging and testing, it is especially important for 
these Taiwanese semiconductor companies to work with familiar cleanroom builders when 
expanding in the US. This is one of the reasons why Taiwanese cleanroom companies are 
receiving overseas orders and starting to set up global bases, in our view. Despite higher 
management, material, and manpower costs in the US, most cleanroom companies are 
benefitting from the trend by improving margins, given the higher complexity and risks to 
operate overseas. Across overseas hubs in Texas and Phoenix (expected to begin from 
2027), Acter prioritises local hiring and partners with regional civil contractors to shorten 
the learning curve and to avoid the high overhead. To further address the potential labour 
shortages in the future, the company has established cross-border academic and 
vocational partnerships to bring Southeast Asian talent to Taiwan for technical training 
before deploying them back to their home markets, securing future human capacity to 
support global expansion projects.

By product, cleanroom electromechanical services remain Acter’s largest source of 
revenue (accounting for 66% of 1H26 revenue, per the company). These services include 
cleanroom architectural and MEP engineering, which powers airflow, filtration, 
environmental regulation and utility controls. Water, gas and chemical delivery systems are 
its second-largest source of revenue (accounting for 24% of 1H26 revenue, per the 
company). Contributed mainly by Novatech (6613 TT, not rated) and Rayzher (7703 TT, not 
rated), these services cover the main systems and hook-up systems, for  ultra-pure water 
(UPW), specialty gases, chemical piping, etc., all of which require highly precise build-up 
work. Except for cleanroom electromechanical and water, gas and chemical delivery 
systems, the rest of Acter’s revenue comes from the consumer industry services 
electromechanical (5%), biomedical (3%), and high-tech equipment and materials sales 
and services (1%).

Acter: revenue by product

Source: Company, Daiwa

Technically, Acter integrates digital transformation and green engineering into its business 
model, helping it differentiate from peers. Acter deploys digital twin technology and 1:1 3D 
BIM modelling to detect piping interferences and optimise spatial layout before installation, 
minimising schedule delays. This makes Acter a more reliable option, in our view, and thus 
being a favourable supplier, especially for the semiconductor clients that have extremely 
high quality and swiftness criterion.

Bright outlook: strong order visibility 
Rising orders on hand due to supply-demand imbalance for cleanrooms 
Acter's order backlog has increased from TWD55bn in 1Q26 to TWD60bn in 2Q26 and 
TWD66bn in July 2026. We expect this figure to further increase in the following quarters, 
given the supply-demand imbalance in the cleanroom sector. This is composed of 
semiconductor (63%), electronic assembly (11%), consumer (9%) and others (17%), per 
the company. In 2H25, the company accelerated the digestion of small-scale projects 
below TWD1bn, and, as a result, starting from 1H26, it successfully shifted its project

Back-end packaging, cloud infrastructure and hardware assembly are additional growth 
drivers. Acter serves as a contractor for both Taiwanese and overseas OSAT providers,

contract obtained from the same client in 2025. In cloud infrastructure, Acter maintains 
qualified supplier status with US CSPs, providing mechanical, electrical and plumbing 
(MEP) integration, especially for those data centres that implement liquid-cooling systems. 
The company also wins factory construction projects for Taiwanese ODMs across the US 
and Southeast Asia, as well as PCB facility projects in Thailand and China. Non-tech 
sectors, including biotechnology, commercial buildings, smart logistics hubs and medical 
facilities, comprise 9% of the total backlog, per management.

Competitive advantages 
High entry barriers to the segment 
Comprehensive product portfolio, empowered by subsidiaries 
Acter provides a one-stop EPCM solution, leveraging its subsidiaries to deliver complete 
solutions. Within the group, the parent company Acter focuses on overall cleanroom and 
MEP system integration; subsidiary Novatech provides primary chemical systems and 
hook-up systems; and sub-subsidiary Rayzer specialises in primary specialty-gas systems 
and hook-up systems. In China, Suzhou Winmax, also a subsidiary of Novatech, focuses 
on the supply of wet processing and chemical handling equipment. This integrated model 
covers cleanroom engineering integration, water, power, chemical and gas-utility systems, 
as well as post-completion validation and maintenance, ensuring cross-system 
compatibility while mitigating risks associated with multi-contractor hiring.

Under global supply chain diversification trends, this unified group strategy strengthens 
order-winning capabilities in overseas markets. Clients expanding internationally face 
regulatory, linguistic and supply-chain complexities, favouring partners that offer one-stop 
services with a proven cross-border track record.

A diverse client base helps Acter weather economic cycles 
Acter’s main competitive advantage is its diversification of clients by industry and region. 
This guides the company through the peaks and troughs of the economy, which is 
especially crucial given the strong cyclicality of the semiconductor/high-tech industry.  Acter 
serves a broad client base across semiconductors, OSAT, PCB, electronics assembly 
(ODM), CSP data centres, biopharmaceuticals and even the consumer industry (including 
commercial real estate and logistics), whereas many peers rely on a few large foundry 
clients. When semiconductor demand slows, capacity expansion in the PCB or data centre 
sectors offset the decline. Additionally, Acter avoids concentration risk and margin 
compression from major foundry accounts, as projects from leading foundries yield lower 
margins; by serving niche markets like US-based CSPs, the company achieves higher 
gross margins.

Geographically, Acter operates across Taiwan, China, Southeast Asia, the US and other 
countries. The company has OSAT and foundry customers in Taiwan and China, PCB 
clients expanding into Thailand, and ODM and data centre clients establishing operations 
in the US. Across different countries, local hiring and local supply chain implementation 
can further reduce costs and increase efficiency relative to peers reliant on a cross-border 
labour force.

Labour shortages 
Amid global labour shortages, Acter minimises the operational impact and turns workforce 
constraints into a moat through localised talent development. Since engineering labour 
cannot easily be automated, Acter has built a cross-border talent pipeline via industry-
academia partnerships. The company recruits Southeast Asian students for specialised 
M&E engineering training in Taiwan, deploying them to global project sites after their 
graduation to solve labour bottlenecks. This structure maintains an engineering team with 
an average tenure of more than 7 years, creating an operational barrier in an industry with 
a high employee turnover rate.

Compared with peers relying on high-cost personnel sent from Taiwan, we note that Acter’s 
local hiring strategy creates cost and efficiency advantages. Prioritising compliant local 
hiring across overseas markets reduces travel expenses while accelerating integration with 
local supply chains and regulations. Localised teams also handle post-completion services 
and technical support directly on-site, building long-term client trust in overseas markets.

The supply of cleanroom 
services is limited due to 
high entry barriers in the 
segment

### Page 11

11

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Numerous competitors, each with different specialisation 
Acter faces different competitors across its main product lines. In the EPCM segment, 
including cleanroom and MEP works, key competitors include UIS (2404 TT, not rated), 
L&K (6139 TT, not rated), Yankey (6691 TT, not rated) and Jiuhan (6903 TT, not rated). 
Despite having business models similar to Acter, UIS, Yankey and Jiuhan focus more on 
major semiconductor players in both Taiwan and the US, and L&K concentrates on ASEAN 
semiconductor projects. In the equipment/material sales and agency space, Acter mainly 
competes with Marketech (6196 TT, not rated). In the water, gas and chemical delivery 
systems area, through Novatech and Rayzher, Acter group competes with Marketech, 
Mega Union (6944 TT, not rated), Taiwan Puritic (6826 TT, not rated), Trusval (6667 TT, not 
rated) and Cica-Huntek (6725 TT, not rated).

In our view, despite the competitive environment, Acter maintains a formidable competitive 
moat built on several key advantages. We attribute this to its more than 45 year experience 
in the industry, enabling it to develop specialised expertise that is hard for competitors to 
replicate.

Financial analysis 
We forecast the company’s net profit to rise at a CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E, driven by 
a revenue CAGR of 24.6% due to surging demand from the high-tech industry (an increase 
in the order backlog to TWD66bn at the end of July 2026 vs. TWD46bn at end-2025) and a 
3.3pp expansion in operating margin over 2026-28E, driven by the gross margin 
improvement from more favourable project wins and operating leverage.

Strong order visibility, with revenue expected to grow 
across all sectors over 2026-28E 
We forecast Acter’s revenue to increase at a CAGR of 24.6% over 2026-28E, driven by 
scale expansion and a growing demand from various industries, including semiconductor, 
electronic assembly, optoelectronics and others.

Acter: revenue growth trend

Gross- and operating-margin expansion trend  
Over 2026-28E, we forecast Acter’s gross and operating margins to expand on rising 
revenue scale and a higher complexity of cleanrooms for advanced manufacturing. We 
note that the gross margin from high-tech cleanrooms is higher than that from consumer 
industries, such as commercial buildings and biopharmaceuticals. Thus, we estimate the 
company’s gross margin to reach 26.0% in 2028E from 18.9% in 2025 and its operating 
margin to reach 21.6% in 2028E from 13.7% in 2025.

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Valuation and risks  
Undemanding valuation, alongside its leading global 
positions in the OSAT and memory segments and an 
optimistic industry outlook  
We initiate coverage of Acter with a Buy (1) rating and 12-month TP of TWD1,415, based 
on a target PER of 20x applied to our one-year-forward EPS forecast. This target multiple 
is higher than the average of its past-3-year 1-year-forward PER of 12.2x (ranged from 
7.4x to 23.5x) and the average of its past-5-year 1-year-forward PER of 9.6x (ranged from 
4.4x to 23.5x), which we view as undemanding as we forecast a 36.9% net profit CAGR 
over 2026-28E, implying a 0.54x PEG. This is supported by its business expansion in the 
next 2-3 years. We see potential for a rerating of the stock, given Acter’s global leadership 
in cleanroom EPCM, particularly in the OSAT and memory industries.

The stock is trading currently at a 15.8x 1-year-forward PER vs. its earnings CAGR of 
36.9% over 2026-28E, translating to a 0.43x PEG ratio. Its share price has underperformed 
TAIEX Index by 32.7% in 3Q26, mainly due to its weaker-than-expected 2Q26 results. 
However, given the potential improvement in sector sentiment following TSMC’s results on 
15 October 2026, along with the likelihood that Acter’s revenue momentum will strengthen 
in the coming months, we recommend that investors buy the stock.

Acter: past-3-year-forward PER (average: 12.0x)    Acter: past-5-year-forward PER (average: 9.5x)

Source: Bloomberg, Daiwa estimates  Source: Bloomberg, Daiwa estimates

We compare Acter with other leading cleanroom EPCM companies such as UIS (2404 TT, 
TWD1,330.0, not rated), L&K (6139 TT, TWD787.0, not rated) and Yankey (6691 TT, 
TWD776.0, not rated). UIS, L&K and Yankey are trading at a PER of 14.7x (earnings 
CAGR of 40.7% over 2025-27E), 9.5x (earnings CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E) and 
15.1x (earnings CAGR of 53.6% over 2025-27E), on their 1-year-forward EPS, 
respectively.

Acter’s peers are trading at 18.7x for 2026E and 14.2x for 2027E. We forecast Acter to 
deliver an EPS CAGR of 36.9% over 2026-28E vs. 31.5% CAGR for the segment over the 
same period.

We believe our target one-year-forward PER of 20x (equivalent to a 0.54x PEG using 
2026-28E EPS CAGR) is undemanding as: 1) Acter is ramping up capacity to meet 
growing demand from high-tech customers and is in an expansion cycle; and 2) its 
leading position among OSAT and memory clients warrants a valuation premium, 
compared to its global peers.

Risks to our call  
Primary/key risks: 
Weaker-than-expected expansion in semiconductor industry 
The biggest downside risk to our call for Acter is weaker-than-expected expansion from 
semiconductor clients, especially in advanced-node foundries, advanced packaging and 
OSAT. If global demand for AI servers, HPC or HBM slows, or if clients delay fab build-ups 
and overseas expansion plans, it will directly pressure order inflows and backlog 
conversion for Acter.

Project execution delays or cancellations 
Acter’s revenue recognition is tied to large, complex EPCM and integrated engineering 
projects, which are often subject to customer-driven delays. Any postponement in project 
Our 12-month TP of 
TWD1,415 is also 
supported by our DCF 
valuation

### Page 18

18

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
schedule, construction bottlenecks or cancellation of large projects could defer revenue 
recognition and hurt overall profit. This risk is more obvious in overseas projects, where 
licensing, fire-safety approvals and hazardous-chemical regulations are more complicated.

Higher-than-expected raw-material price hikes 
Raw-material costs (especially copper and oil-related costs) account for 50-60% of COGS 
and thus the price volatility makes costs difficult to manage. Acter needs to maintain 
supplier relations, negotiate appropriate price-adjustment mechanisms with clients, and 
invest in R&D to identify composite material substitutes, thereby reducing reliance on any 
single component.

Secondary risks:  
Labour issues or larger-than-expected increases in labour costs  
Cleanroom engineering is highly labour-intensive and relies on on-site installation and 
supervision. Although Acter has built a local hiring and talent-development model, a larger-
than-expected shortage of engineers or rising wage pressure could still raise costs and 
constrain capacity expansion. Any inability to secure enough skilled labour may also limit 
the company’s ability to convert orders into revenue in time.

Margin pressure from lower-margin projects or mix shift 
Acter’s business mix includes a few low-margin C&S services as well as larger EPCM and 
system integration contracts. If the company takes on a higher proportion of lower-margin 
projects, or if competition intensifies in certain geographies or customer segments, gross 
margin could come under pressure. In addition, an unfavourable product mix shift towards 
less profitable work could offset benefits from backlog growth.

Overseas expansion and regulatory risks 
Acter is expanding across markets in the US, Southeast Asia, Japan and China, which 
exposes it to FX movements, local compliance requirements, tax complexity and regulatory 
uncertainty. Delays in licensing, changes in safety standards or local-content requirements 
could increase project costs and reduce execution efficiency. Any misstep in managing 
overseas operations could damage profitability and client retention. 
Key risks to our call: 
weaker-than-expected 
expansion in 
semiconductor industry, 
project delays or 
cancellations, and 
higher-than-expected 
raw-material price hikes

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Appendix 
Company background 
History 
Acter (5536 TT), founded in 1979 and headquartered in Taichung, Taiwan, is a cleanroom 
EPCM provider with over four decades of experience. Starting with early mechanical and 
electrical (M&E) engineering through vertical and horizontal integration, Acter has now 
developed into an engineering technology group with cross-border execution capabilities. A 
cleanroom building process begins with civil & structural (C&S) works to establish the 
physical plant, followed by mechanical, electrical, and plumbing (MEP) works for 
essential utility generation, where Acter is one of the experts. After MEP is finished, 
chemicals/gas/water are then distributed via main systems through primary piping 
networks, and finally delivered to production equipment through hook-up systems. The 
company’s core competency is providing one-stop integrated total solutions, spanning 
services, primary piping networks and hook-up systems, leaving C&S services to other 
engineering peers, given the nature of the latter segment’s low margin and long timespan.

In terms of business model, Acter delivers EPCM (Engineering, Procurement, 
Construction, Management) capabilities. From initial customised planning and feasibility 
assessments to strategic procurement, installation, and post-completion maintenance, 
Acter builds production environments tailored to client process requirements.

Acter: EPCM solutions offered

Source: Company, Daiwa

Regarding global expansion, Acter actively responds to the global supply chain’s “N+1” 
strategy, operating across 11 regions with 33 companies globally. Its service network spans 
Taiwan, China, Southeast Asia (including Thailand, Malaysia, Vietnam, Indonesia, and 
Singapore), Japan, India, and the US. The company leverages a group synergy strategy 
by integrating resources from key subsidiaries – such as Novatech (6613 TT, not rated; 
provides chemical primary and hook-up systems), Rayzher (7703 TT, not rated; provides 
special gas primary and hook-up supply systems), and Acter Group (603163 CH, not rated; 
provides cleanroom engineering in China) – to form a complete technical ecosystem, 
ensuring cross-border capability and quality.

Comprehensive 
cleanroom EPCM 
provider in Taiwan; 
dominates both high-
tech market and 
consumer industry

### Page 20

20

Acter (5536 TT): 7 October 2026 
Acter: global footprint

Source: Company, Daiwa

In 2Q26, Acter classified revenue as: cleanroom electromechanical (63%), water, gas and 
chemical delivery systems (28%), consumer industry electromechanical (6%), biomedical 
(2%) and high-tech equipment & materials sales & services (1%). In 2025, the product mix 
was: cleanroom electromechanical (71%), water, gas and chemical delivery systems 
(20%), consumer industry electromechanical (4%), biomedical (2%) and high-tech 
equipment & materials sales & services (3%).

Acter serves a diversified client base divided into high-tech and consumer divisions. The 
high-tech division focuses on advanced sectors including semiconductors, OSAT, PCB, AI 
data centres (for CSPs), and electronics assembly. The consumer division extends into 
healthcare, premium commercial properties, logistics facilities, and green engineering. This 
multi-industry strategy allows the company to capitalise on tech sector upgrades while 
maintaining resilient operations across technology (especially semiconductor) cycles.

Acter adopts a management strategy involving local hiring in overseas markets, prioritising 
collaboration with local supply chains and compliance with local regulations to reduce 
cross-border deployment costs and thus build strong local teams. Acter has more than 
2,600 employees worldwide (average age: 42 years; average length of service: 7 years). 
With more than 45 years of experience in EPCM, the company plays a reliable role for both 
high-tech and consumer industry.

Executive/board
quality 1

review security implementation.
Capital management 1
Acter manages capital in a stable and shareholder-friendly manner. Over the past several years,
Acter has typically maintained a dividend payout ratio of 65-80%. It also maintains a strong
financing network across multiple banks, supporting flexibility for project execution and
expansion while preserving financial stability.
Related party &
transaction 1
Acter’s business is supported by a transparent network of suppliers, project partners, and

modeling, which improve design accuracy, construction efficiency, and project consistency.

floors after establishing paper segregation and reuse procedures. The company also promoted
sustainability measures through emissions inventory management, environmental disclosures,
and resource-efficiency practices. These actions show active environmental management and a
clear commitment to lowering operational impact.

Document 272: Marvell 營收翻十倍？SpaceX 下場做電信商？Google帶核電飛？ - KP思考筆記(第63期)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Marvell 營收翻十倍？SpaceX 下場做電信商？Google帶核電飛？ - KP思考筆記(第63期).pdf

### Page 1

From:    KP - FOMO 研究院  <fomosoc@substack.com>
Subject: Marvell 營收翻⼗倍？ SpaceX 下場做電信商？ Google 帶核電⾶？  - KP 思考筆記 ( 第 63 期 )
Date:    5 hours ago
Marvell 營收翻⼗倍？ SpaceX 下場做電信商？ Google 帶核電⾶？  - KP 思考筆記 ( 第 63 期 )
Forwarded this email? Subscribe here for more
Marvell 營收翻⼗倍？ SpaceX 下場
做電信商？ Google 帶核電⾶？  - KP
思考筆記 ( 第 63 期 )
KP@FOMOSOC, BENITA HSU, AND ポニョ  オタク
OCT 10
READ IN APP
⼤家好，我是  KP ，歡迎來到第  63 期的《週末思考筆記》。
⼤概兩個⽉前，我開了⼀個新欄⽬，叫「財報解剖」。當時有跟⼤家講，這是
⼀個新嘗試，不保證更新頻率，先跑跑看。

兩個⽉下來，總共出了五篇，節奏慢慢穩了，我和團隊也越寫越順。所以今天
想正式跟⼤家宣佈：這個欄⽬會⼀直做下去。
也就是說， FOMO 研究院現在有三條線：
深⼊分析： ⼀個產業或公司主題，深挖到底，週中發出；

### Page 2

思考筆記： 相對短⼀點，⼀週的市場觀察與隨筆，週末發出；
財報解剖： ⽐較硬核，數字會多⼀點，在⼀家公司出財報之後發出。
講到這裡，我想多聊幾句我對這三條線的想法。
深⼊分析做了超過⼀年了。這⼀年多，我們⼀起⾛過了很多產業、很多公司，
有些可能你之前完全沒聽過，有些你可能聽過但沒認真看過。
我很喜歡這個過程，把⼀個陌⽣的東⻄拆開，搞懂它，再跟你講清楚。
但我最近開始有⼀個感覺：廣度鋪開了，是時候多⼀點往深度⾛。
財報解剖做的事情，其實跟深⼊分析不太⼀樣。
深⼊分析是帶你認識⼀個你可能不知道的公司或產業；財報解剖是挑⼀家⼤家
都聽過的公司，但我們不只看⼀次，我們持續看、反覆看、每個季度再看⼀
次。
我想做的事情是建⽴⼀個「追蹤名單」。你作為讀者，可以每個季度回來，看
看這家公司跟上⼀季⽐怎麼樣了、跟我們上次拆解的時候⽐怎麼樣了。
⽼實講，我⽬前還沒有把同⼀家公司寫過兩次財報解剖，所以這個「追蹤」的
感覺到底會變成什麼樣⼦，我⾃⼰也很期待，要跑起來才知道。
但⽅向是這樣的：⼀個覆蓋名單，慢慢成形，⼤家可以⼀起追蹤那些公司的進

展。
完整名單我還沒完全定下來，之後會慢慢跟⼤家揭曉。
我也不保證每⼀次財報我都會寫⼀篇完整的⻑⽂，有些季度可能真的沒什麼⼤
變化，但我希望⾄少會有⼀個短筆記，跟你講⼀聲：「嘿，我看了，⼤概是這
樣。」
這樣你不會斷線，我也能把脈搏持續摸著。
好，講完願景，講⼀下實際的節奏調整。
下週有兩家公司出財報（我不講名字，讓⼤家猜⼀下，但你查⼀下財報⽇曆⼤
概猜得到），我和團隊會集中⽕⼒準備，財報出來之後也會第⼀時間拆解。
所以下週，會有兩篇財報解剖，取代原本的深⼊分析。

### Page 3

往後也是這樣：如果某⼀周有⽐較多我們要覆蓋的財報，財報解剖可能會頂掉
那週的深⼊分析。每次有這個情況，我會提前跟⼤家講⼀聲。
我想強調的是，這不是說深⼊分析不重要，它依然是主菜。
只是跑了⼀年多，我們把地圖畫得夠⼤了，接下來我想把⼀些已經畫上地
圖的地⽅，再鑽深⼀點。
與其每週都在開新地圖，不如偶爾回到⼀個⽼朋友那裡，看看它這三個⽉
⻑成什麼樣⼦了。
做「深⼊分析」需要極⼤的腦⼒激盪，但我後來發現，其實⼤家的注意⼒也是
有限且寶貴的資源。
如果我們每週都在塞⼀個全新的、陌⽣的公司或產業，久了⼤家也會產⽣資訊
疲勞。「怎麼這週⼜是⼀個我沒聽過的名字？」。
所以，偶爾⽤⼤家熟悉的公司（財報解剖）來替換⼀下，不僅能讓我的研究團
隊有更充⾜的時間打磨品質，也能讓⼤家在閱讀時有更熟悉的錨點，讀起來更
輕鬆、更有共鳴，也有⼀種「喔，原來它變成這樣了」的樂趣。
FOMO 研究院電⼦報  is a reader-supported
publication. To receive new posts and support

my work, consider becoming a free or paid
subscriber.
Subscribed
這個星期我們有三個主題，但當中有兩個⽐較⻑：
主題⼀  | Marvell 說五年後營收會翻⼗倍？
主題⼆  | Google 簽約  Constellation Energy ，核電準備起⾶？
主題三  | SpaceX 準備下場做電信商？
不妨考慮先讚後看，我們開始 !

### Page 4

主題⼀  | Marvell 說五年後營收會翻
⼗倍？
Marvell 的投資⼈⽇上，管理層把  2031 年的營收⽬標畫在了  700 億到  900 億美
元之間，中位數  800 億。這是現在  82 億營收的整整⼗倍。
華爾街之前怎麼估的？⼤約  470 億。
「 800 億」這個⼤餅當然很吸引，但背後是由甚麼組成才是重要的。
我想和⼤家分享三個洞察。
洞察⼀：光通訊是「本命基本盤」，客製化只是「⾼槓桿期權」
在  Marvell 規劃的  2031 年  800 億美元營收藍圖中，我們可以把業務拆成兩⼤核
⼼：
( 其實還有交與儲存，通訊等等，但這些⽐較⼩，我們暫不討論 )

### Page 5

1. 互連與光通訊（ Interconnect ）： 預估營收  375 億美元。對⽐  2030 年該領
域  650 億美元的市場規模（ TAM ），這意味著  Marvell 預期⾃⼰將拿下⾼
達  58% 的市場份額。這是⼀種「準壟斷」地位。
2. 客製化晶⽚（ Custom Silicon ）： 預估營收  300 億美元。看起來數字也很
⼤，但對⽐  2030 年⾼達  2,350 億美元的客製化晶⽚市場， Marvell 的份額
其實只有  13% 。
雖然我在不同⽂章也說過很多次，但總是很多⼈仍然視 Marvell 為⼀家 ASIC 公
司。
但在客製化晶⽚市場裡， Marvell 依然只是⼀個挑戰  Broadcom 霸權的「挑戰
者」；但在光通訊與互連領域，它才是領導者。
什麼是互連？在⼀座⼤型  AI 資料中⼼裡⾯，擺滿了成千上萬顆  AI 晶⽚。

### Page 6

每⼀顆晶⽚就算運算速度再快，如果晶⽚跟晶⽚之間傳遞資料太慢，整座機房
就只能停下來空等。 Marvell 賣的產品，就是讓這些晶⽚能夠⾼速互相溝通的
關鍵零件。
這些零件包含了許多類型：例如「光訊號處理晶⽚（ DSP ）」，負責把電訊號轉
換成光訊號，再透過光纖傳送到另⼀個伺服器機櫃；「交換器晶⽚」，負責指揮
資料流量該往哪裡⾛；還有專⾨⽤來增強銅線訊號品質的晶⽚。
根據  Marvell 內部的預估，在伺服器機櫃內部、晶⽚與晶⽚之間互相傳輸的資
料，就佔了整座  AI 資料中⼼總流量的  85% 以上。
如果我們進⼀步把這兩組數據的「成⻑率」放在⼀起⽐對，會發現⼀個更值得
玩味的細節：
互連產品： Marvell 預估⾃家的營收年成⻑率約為  60% 到  70% ，⽽整個市
場的成⻑率⼤約也是  65% 。這意味著， Marvell 的預測前提並不是「我要
去搶下更多市場」，⽽是「我要在未來五年內，穩穩守住這過半的市占
率」。
客製化晶⽚： Marvell 預估⾃家的營收年成⻑率⾼達  75% 到  85% ，遠遠⾼

於整個市場  55% 的平均成⻑速度。這意味著， Marvell 的預測前提是「我
要發動猛烈進攻，從競爭對⼿的⼿中搶下更多的市占率」。
⼀守⼀攻之間，⾯臨的⾵險完全是兩回事。
在防守的這⼀邊（互連產品），公司最害怕的是「技術世代交替」。互連產品是
賣給所有客戶的標準化產品，不依賴單⼀買家。
但是，只要傳輸速度每提升⼀個世代（例如從  800G 到  1.6T ，再到  3.2T ），客
戶都會重新評估並選擇供應商。

### Page 7

每當技術進⼊世代交替的時候，原本穩固的市占率就有可能被競爭對⼿趁虛⽽
⼊。
在進攻的這⼀邊（客製化晶⽚），潛在客戶池本來就只有那幾家⼤型雲端服務
商。只要其中⼀家客戶決定把下⼀代產品的訂單轉給別⼈，或是決定把產品延
後⼀代推出，營收就會瞬間掉⼀⼤塊。
所以， Marvell 喊出的  800 億美元營收⽬標，是由兩個完全不同的賭注拼湊
⽽成的。
⼀個要求它在五年內絕對不能失守陣地，另⼀個則要求它在五年內必須持
續搶勝。這兩個條件必須同時成⽴，總⽬標才有可能順利兌現。
這⼀點在短期的業績調升中展現得淋漓盡致。
Marvell 將  2028 財年的營收⽬標從  180 億美元調升⾄  200 億美元，這多出來的
20 億美元，完全是由光通訊（ Scale-out/Scale-up optics ）和交換器晶⽚拉動
的，跟客製化晶⽚沒有關係。
正如執⾏⻑  Matt Murphy 所說：「是 Interconnect 在引領這場競賽。」
你買  Marvell ，買的其實是  AI 時代「數據傳輸」的物理剛需，客製化晶⽚

更多是附贈的超額期權。
還有⼀個現在幾乎是零、但可能改變遊戲的東⻄： scale-up 光學。
今天這塊的收⼊基本是零。管理層說放量是  2028 年的事。每顆  XPU 的光學元
件價值是數千美元，⽽且鏈路的兩端都需要光學。
Celestial AI 的光⼦互連技術，瞄準的是記憶體共享： HBM 距離處理器⼤約  1.5
毫⽶，所以今天加記憶體基本等於加處理器。
洞察⼆：⽑利率的障眼法，營業利益率才是真功夫
在展開之前，我們先把兩個名詞講清楚。因為接下來這段分析，全部建⽴在這
個區別上。
⽑利率  (Gross Margin)，是營收減去「直接製造成本」之後的⽐例。
所謂直接製造成本，就是晶圓、封裝、測試這些跟「做出這顆晶⽚」直接相關
的費⽤。它不包含研發、銷售、⾏政這些間接開銷。

### Page 8

營業利益率  (Operating Margin)，是把所有營運費⽤都扣完之後，真正剩下的
利潤⽐例。
除了直接製造成本，它還要扣掉研發費⽤、銷售費⽤、⾏政費⽤，這才是衡量
⼀家公司「到底賺不賺錢」的真實指標。
⼀句話總結：⽑利率告訴你「產品本⾝的利潤空間有多⼤」，營業利益率告訴
你「公司在扣完所有開銷之後，真正賺到⼿多少」。
現在，回到  Marvell 。
如果你有關注上⼀季的財報，你可能會記得：第三季的⽑利率指引從  58.9% 微
幅下滑到  57.5% 到  58.5% 。就這麼⼀點點，股價當天重挫了  10% 。
市場的解讀是，⽑利率降了，代表產品利潤在縮⽔，代表⽣意變差了，賣。
但這個結論並不是完全正確的。因為⽑利率下降的原因，不是產品利潤變薄
了，是「營收組合變了」。
這就要講到  Marvell 兩種產品的本質差異。
FOMO 研究院電⼦報  is a reader-supported
publication. To receive new posts and support

my work, consider becoming a free or paid
subscriber.
Subscribed
第⼀種：標準光通訊產品。⽑利率⾼，但研發費⽤重。
這類產品是  Marvell ⾃⼰設計、⾃⼰開發，然後賣給很多客戶。因為是標準化
產品，每顆晶⽚的直接製造成本相對低，所以⽑利率很⾼。
但代價是， Marvell 必須⾃⼰養⼀⼤批⼯程師，持續投⼊下⼀代產品的研發。
這些研發費⽤是  Marvell ⾃⼰承擔的，全部算在營業費⽤裡。
第⼆種：客製化晶⽚。⽑利率較低，但營業利益率⾮常好。
客製化晶⽚的直接製造成本⽐較⾼，因為要針對每個客戶的需求做定制化設計
和驗證，所以⽑利率看起來⽐較低。
但這裡有⼀個關鍵的結構差異：客製化專案的研發費⽤，⼤部分是由客戶承擔
的。業界叫  NRE （ Non-Recurring Engineering ），也就是客戶在專案啟動時就先
付⼀筆研發費。

### Page 9

這意味著， Marvell 做客製化晶⽚的時候，最貴的那塊「研發」，已經被客戶買
單了。
⼀旦進⼊量產，規模效應出來，每⼀顆晶⽚的邊際成本很低，不需要再額外投
⼊⼤量研發。
所以結果是：⽑利率⽐較低，但扣完所有費⽤之後，真正賺到⼿裡的利潤⽐例
反⽽更⾼。
現在你明⽩上次財報為什麼跌了。
當客製化晶⽚的營收佔⽐提⾼，整體⽑利率⾃然會往下⾛。因為客製化的⽑利
率⽐標準產品低。
但這不代表⽣意變差了。這代表營收正在從「⾼⽑利但研發⾃付」的產品，轉
向「低⽑利但研發由客戶買單」的產品。⽑利率表⾯上掉了，但營業利益率其
實在改善。
這次投資⼈⽇，管理層直接回答了這個問題。
他們沒有提⾼⽑利率的⻑期⽬標。它維持在  56% 到  59% 。意思是：⽑利率會
隨著產品組合波動，這是結構性的。
但他們把營業利益率的⻑期⽬標，從過去的  38% 到  40% ，⼀⼝氣拉到了  44%
到  46% 。整整多了六個百分點。
邏輯很簡單：當營收從  82 億成⻑到  800 億，研發團隊不會變成⼗倍⼤，銷售
團隊不會變成⼗倍⼤，⾏政費⽤不會變成⼗倍⼤。營業費⽤的增速，⼤約只有

營收增速的⼀半。

### Page 10

這就叫營運槓桿。規模越⼤，每⼀塊錢營收裡⾯，真正掉進股東⼝袋的⽐例就
越⾼。
管理層甚⾄給了⼀個對沖框架：
如果未來客製化晶⽚賣得⽐預期好，⽑利率會往  56% 那⼀端靠攏，但營業利益
率會被撐住，因為研發費是客戶出的。
如果光通訊賣得⽐預期好，⽑利率會往  59% 那⼀端⾛，但研發費⽤會⽐較重，
會吃掉⼀些。
不管哪⼀邊贏，營業利益率都應該守在  44% 到  46% 。
因此，最重要的數字，是營業利益率有沒有守住。
⽽那個「 30 in 30 」，也就是  2030 年  EPS 超過  30 美元，只有在這個營運槓桿真
的兌現的情況下才成⽴。
800 億的營收聽起來很厲害。但如果營業利益率守不住，那  800 億就只是營
收很⼤，結果⼤部分都拿去養⼯程師  and 付⾏政費⽤了，股東分不到多
少。
洞察三： Google 的  1200 億合約，暫時觀察就好
市場上另⼀個焦點，是媒體紛紛報導的「 Marvell 與  Google 簽署六年、總⾦額
1,200 億美元的龐⼤合作訂單」。

### Page 11

這個數字聽起來很嚇⼈。但如果你去翻今年  8 ⽉  18 ⽇  Marvell 提交給美國證管
會（ SEC ）的原始公告，會發現故事完全不是你想的那樣。
這份公告的真實內容是： Marvell 發放了⼀批「認股權證」給  Google 。這個權
利允許  Google 在未來，以每股  206.58 美元的約定價格，購買最多約  5,897 萬
股的  Marvell 股票。
但關鍵是，這批認股權證不是⼀次性給的。
除了第⼀年按季度發放的⼀⼩部分之外，其餘絕⼤部分被拆分成了  240 批。規
則很簡單： Google 每向  Marvell 採購滿  5 億美元的客製化產品，才可以解鎖其
中⼀批。
240 批，乘以每批  5 億美元，算出來的結果正好就是  1,200 億美元。
所以這個數字的真實含義是：如果  Google 想要拿滿所有的認股權證，它必須
先累計採購到  1,200 億美元。這是解鎖的「上限」，不是⼀張已經簽字畫押的採
購訂單。
換個⾓度想，這個合約結構其實透露了⼀個很有意思的事實：Marvell 需要拿

出⾃家公司的股票作為商業誘因，才能吸引  Google 在未來持續多下訂單。
⽽管理層在投資⼈⽇上的說法也⾮常誠實。他們表⽰， 2031 年的財務預測中，
只納⼊了這筆合作的「⼀⼩部分」。⽽且他們強調：「即便未來沒有拿到任何新
的設計案，也能穩穩落在  700 到  900 億美元的預測區間內。」
這句話的意思是：那  1,200 億的天花板，根本不在  2031 年的預測模型裡。它是
⼀個更⻑期的可能性，不是眼前的保證。
不過，這裡有⼀個細節必須釐清。
管理層在投資⼈⽇上強調的是「沒有任何單⼀  socket 超過營收的  10% 」，⽽不
是「沒有任何單⼀客戶」。
Socket 是⼀顆晶⽚在客戶系統裡佔的那個位⼦（設計席位）。同⼀個客戶可以
有很多個  socket 。⽐如  Amazon ，它的下⼀代  Trainium 、光  DSP 、交換器、機
房間互連，加起來就是四個不同的  socket 。
所以，「單⼀  socket 不超過  10% 」，只代表沒有「單獨⼀顆晶⽚」⼤到能決定全

年營收。這並不意味著客戶集中度降低了， Amazon 依然是  Marvell 最⼤的客
戶。
Marvell 真正在做的，是「在同⼀個⼤客戶裡，塞進更多不同種類的晶⽚」。他
們現在已經同時向美國四⼤超⼤規模數據中⼼出貨客製化晶⽚，並且在每個客

### Page 12

戶內部贏下了多個設計席位。
客製化業務已經從「靠單⼀產品押寶」，⾛向了「⽤產品矩陣深度綁定」。這才
是它作為期權最真實的狀態。

真正的信號：⼗個⽉，四次上調
不過⽼實說，現在距離 2031 年還有 5 年。在 AI 時代， 5 個⽉也嫌太多。任何遠期
預測，往往只是基於「 AI 資本⽀出會無限膨脹」的樂觀外推。
所以 2031 年的預測數字，聽聽就好。
真正能驗證  Marvell 底氣的，不是  2031 年的  800 億，⽽是過去⼗個⽉內，
管理階層將  2028 年的營收⽬標連續調升了四次（從  130 億、 150 億、 180
億，⼀路拔⾼到現在的  200 億）。
這種短期的、⾼頻率的預期上修，是無法靠吹⽜粉飾的。它背後代表的是實打

實、正在出貨的光模組與交換器晶⽚。
Marvell 賣的不是「另⼀家定制  ASIC 廠商」的故事。

### Page 13

它賣的是：是⼀個互聯的霸主，加上⼀張在定制市場裡搶份額的期權，再加上
⼀個因為規模效應⽽重新定價的利潤率模型。
「 Marvell 2031 年要做到  800 億」，這句話的解讀應該是：那  800 億裡⾯，多少
是你已經看⾒的，多少是你必須相信的？
答案的⽐例，決定了你願意付多少倍。
Marvell 應該如何解讀，我早在半年前， Marvell 還沒爆升的時候和⼤家
深⼊分析過，裡⾯的邏輯仍然成⽴，有興趣的朋友不妨重溫分析。

⽐ NVDA 還⾼？光通訊是 Marvell 重要

想像⼀下，你正坐在 Google 或 Meta 的基礎設施決策會議裡。桌上擺著今年剛被
董事會批准的龐⼤資本⽀出（ CapEx ）預算：⼀個⾼達千多億美元的鉅額數
字。

### Page 14

主題⼆  | Google 簽約
Constellation Energy ，核電準備
起⾶？
當  Google 宣布砸下  43 億美元跟  Constellation Energy 合作時，你可能第⼀個反
應是：核電公司終於要起⾶了嗎？
但事實上， Google 這次砸下重⾦，是去讓 Constellation Energy 升級  11 座現有的
核電機組，預計在  2028 到  2032 年間「憑空擠出」 890 MW 的發電容量。
這在產業裡有個專有名詞，叫做「核電升級擴容（ Nuclear Uprates ）」。
如果把能源產業⽐喻成房地產，現在市場上最值錢的，早就不是⼀塊什麼都沒
有、需要重新整地、環評、等上  15 年才能蓋好的荒地。
現在最值錢的，是⼀棟：⼟地早就買好、建照早就拿到、⽔電管線全部接
妥、裡⾯甚⾄已經住滿房客，⽽且頂樓「居然還能合法再加蓋三層樓」的
現成⼤樓。
這多出來的「三層樓」，就是  AI 巨頭們現在不惜代價、⽤  20 年⻑約也要搶下
來的⿈⾦資產。

時間，是  AI 擴張最⼤的敵⼈
為什麼  Google 選擇升級舊廠，⽽⾮蓋新廠？因為蓋新核電廠最⼤的敵⼈從來
不是技術，⽽是時間。

### Page 15

美國喬治亞州的  Vogtle 核電廠新增兩個反應爐，從動⼯到商轉整整花了⼗五
年，建造成本更是天⽂數字。
相⽐之下，我們來算⼀筆帳： Google 這次幫  CEG 出資  43 億美元換取  890 MW
的新增容量，折算下來，每千⽡（ kW ）的建置成本⼤約是  4,800 美元。
這個數字，僅僅是  Vogtle 新建機組單位成本的幾分之⼀，⽽且速度快了近⼗
年。
「升級擴容」這種特定的增量⽅式，既便宜，⼜剛好能趕上  2028 到  2032 年這
段最致命的缺電窗⼝。
缺電有多急迫？負責管理美國多州電網的  PJM 在最新報告中預估， 2028 到
2029 年就會出現⼤約  6.8 吉⽡（ GW ）的可靠電⼒缺⼝，⽽資料中⼼正是主要
來源。
但在  PJM 最近⼀輪的容量拍賣中，全新機組加上擴容項⽬，最終竟然只成功出
清了約  525 MW 。
⾯對  6.8 GW 的無底深淵，這點新增電⼒根本是杯⽔⾞薪。資料中⼼的擴張速
度，不可能乖乖等上⼗五年。
過去，核電業⾯臨最⼤的難題是前期投資太龐⼤。

如果你要砸  40 億美元升級設備，董事會⼀定會問：未來⼆⼗年的電價怎麼
算？如果五年後電價⼤跌，這筆錢就⽩花了。
現在， Google 和  Amazon 站出來了。他們直接簽下⼗五到⼆⼗年的⻑期購電合
約，把未來的訂單提前包下來。
就在  Google 宣布這筆交易的前⼀週， Amazon 才剛跟  Constellation Energy 達成
另⼀筆包含  190 百萬⽡升級項⽬的⻑約。兩家巨頭加起來，實質⽀持了超過  1
吉⽡的核電增量。
這等同於科技巨頭⽤⾃⾝的信⽤，幫核電業者⼤幅降低了投資⾵險。原本在試
算表上內部報酬率不夠漂亮、不值得投資的升級專案，瞬間變成了華爾街最愛
的穩定獲利資產。
AI 不僅帶來了電⼒需求，更徹底改變了核電產業的融資⽅式。
看懂「升級潛⼒」這個新指標
如果這個邏輯成⽴，⾦融市場對核電公司的估值⽅式也要跟著改寫。

### Page 16

以前評估⼀家核電營運商，⼤家看的是總發電量、電價和燃料成本。但未來，
有⼀個更重要的指標叫做「升級擴容潛⼒」。
市場會開始問：你⼿上的反應爐還可以再增加多少發電量？每增加⼀度電需要
多少成本？美國核管會核准需要多久？你能不能找到像  Google 這種⼤客戶簽
⻑約？
假設有兩家公司，各⾃擁有  10 吉⽡的核電容量。 A 公司的機組已經操到技術
極限，沒有升級餘地。
B 公司的機組還能再擴容  800 百萬⽡，⽽且執照能⼀路延⻑到  2050 年。在現
在的市場眼中，這兩家公司的經濟價值絕對是天壤之別。

反向銷售 Gemini
這筆交易另⼀個有趣的點在於：Google 同時是客戶，也是技術供應商。
在簽下購電協議的同時， CEG 也與  Google 簽署了為期  5 年的技術合作，導⼊

Google Cloud 與  Gemini Enterprise 。⽽  Gemini 會在三個領域使⽤：
加速電⼒開發、選址與併網規劃。
優化核電設備的健康監測與停機維護。
保護電網與核電設施的關鍵營運系統。
Google 拿著⼤筆資⾦去買電，轉過⾝來，⼜把⾃⼰的  AI 與雲端服務賣進了能
源產業最核⼼、最敏感、也最不容出錯的營運系統裡。
⼀旦  Gemini 成功在  CEG 的  11 座核電機組中證明了⾃⼰能優化停機時間、預
測設備故障，這套系統將成為  Google Cloud 攻佔全球公⽤事業市場最強⼤的活
廣告。
升級不是無限魔法

### Page 17

不過，核電升級擴容是有天花板的。你不可能把⼀座  1 吉⽡的反應爐，⼀路改
造成  2 吉⽡。
美國核管會將升級分為不同類型，規模最⼤的擴展功率升級，最多也只能增加
約  20% 的發電量，⽽且需要⼤規模設備改造。
這意味著，當市場上那些最容易升級的反應爐都被榨乾之後，下⼀個需要的百
萬⽡電⼒，最終還是得靠蓋新核電廠、天然氣，或是再⽣能源搭配儲能來補
⾜。
所以，在觀察核電產業時，我們可以把公司分成兩種。⼀種是只靠著核能題材
在說故事的公司，另⼀種是⼿上真的握有可以變現、且還有升級空間的反應爐
的實⼒派。
這場由  AI 驅動的核能復興確實正在發⽣。
但最直接的贏家，是那些在⼆、三⼗年前蓋好、以前⼤家覺得無聊透頂，
現在卻能替  AI 時代擠出關鍵電⼒的舊核電廠。
我之前寫過兩篇電⼒的分析，分別分析了公⽤電⼒發電商，以及獨⽴
發電商的邏輯。⽽在獨⽴發電商中，⼜再分成不同的種類。有興趣的
朋友不妨重溫分析。
核能復興應該買甚麼？獨⽴發電商如何分
析？ - 深⼊分析第 42 期： CEG ， VST ，

TLN ， OKLO ( 電⼒系列下集 )
KP@FOMOSOC · APR 8

### Page 18

2024 年  9 ⽉，三哩島（ Three Mile Island ）核電廠， Constellation Energy 的執⾏
⻑ Joe Dominguez 站在這個曾經因為  1979 年美國史上最嚴重核災⽽舉世聞名、
甚⾄帶有幾分禁忌⾊彩的地⽅，向全世界宣布了⼀項重要的決定：
Read full story
主題三： SpaceX 準備下場做電信
商？
美國⼈這幾⼗年來⽤的⼿機⾨號服務，基本上都離不開三家⼤公司： AT&T 、
Verizon 和  T-Mobile 。
這三巨頭花了幾⼗年的時間來建設⾏動網路，砸⼤錢買下⼤量的無線電頻譜、
蓋基地台、鋪設光纖網路，築起了⼀道外⼈很難跨越的超⾼競爭⾨檻。
不過，兩天前， SpaceX 和  Grain Management 達成協議，準備買下對⽅⼿中的
⼀批  800 MHz 低頻頻譜。
這批頻譜最寬的郡⼤約有  14 MHz 的成對頻段，部分地區甚⾄只有  5 MHz 左
右。整筆交易還需要經過  FCC 核准，⽬前尚未完成交割。

根據媒體報導，這筆交易⼤約花了  80 億美元。消息⼀出，這三⼤電信巨頭的
股價在盤後全⾯⼤跌，跌幅達到  6% 到  7% 。
為什麼只是買下⼀段看不⾒的無線電波頻率，就會讓這些傳統電信商的市值瞬
間⼤縮⽔？ SpaceX ⼜在準備甚麼策略？

### Page 19

要搞懂這件事，我們得先了解  SpaceX 過去到底還缺了什麼武器，以及它未來
打算怎麼打這場仗。
從「補死⾓」到「搶客戶」
⼤家都聽過的星鏈（ Starlink ），是靠著低軌道衛星來提供網路服務。
⼀開始，你想⽤星鏈的網路，還得在⾃家裝個專⾨的接收盤。後來  SpaceX 推
出了「 Direct-to-Cell 」技術，讓⼀般的智慧型⼿機直接連上衛星，不⽤再另外
花錢買傳統那種笨重的衛星電話。
這聽起來超厲害，但它⼀開始的商業⽤途，主要是為了補救傳統基地台訊號打
不到的地⽅。
舉例來說，當你開⾞經過荒郊野外的公路，附近半個基地台都沒有的時候，⼿
機還是可以靠天上衛星傳求救訊息或是簡訊。
所以，早期  SpaceX 其實是跟電信商  T-Mobile 合作的。 T-Mobile 繼續賺客戶的
⽉租費，⽽  SpaceX 則是幫忙填補網路訊號的死⾓。
這原本是⼀個雙贏的安排，⼤家各取所需。
但問題是，死⾓批發和⾃⼰賣⾨號，是兩種完全不同的⽣意。
前者賺的是供應商的錢。⽤戶每⽉付給  T-Mobile ， SpaceX 只是在基地台打不到

的地⽅把簡訊和少量數據接上衛星。
客戶名單、品牌、⽉租定價、加價賣⽅案的權⼒，通通都在  T-Mobile ⼿上。衛
星⽤得越多，越像⼀項被包進電信商資費裡的功能，越不像⼀⾨獨⽴⽣意。
T-Mobile 的執⾏⻑甚⾄公開說過，衛星覆蓋以後會變成標配，不再是差異化。
⾔下之意就是，配⾓的批發費，遲早會隨著這件事變便宜。
後者賺的才是電信商的錢。⾨號⽣意的核⼼在於你擁有客戶、擁有帳單、擁有
定價權。
問題來了， SpaceX 現在已經不⽢於只當個配⾓。
今年  8 ⽉， SpaceX 的總裁  Gwynne Shotwell 很明⽩地表⽰，公司有意⾃⼰蓋地
⾯網路，把美國無線市場估在⼤約  6,000 億美元，直接跟⼤型電信商正⾯對
決。

### Page 20

到了  10 ⽉， SpaceX 更在官⽅公告裡放話，Starlink Mobile 的最終⽬標，就是
要成為美國主要的⼿機電信業者。
這代表  SpaceX 想做的，早就超出了「讓你在深⼭裡能收到⼀格訊號」的範
圍。
它是希望有⼀天，你可以直接打電話退掉  Verizon 的⽉租⽅案，改向  Starlink
Mobile 繳錢辦⼿機⾨號。
當「合作夥伴」公開宣⽰要「搶客戶」的時候，雙⽅的關係就產⽣了質
變。
電信商的防禦同盟
要理解這場博弈，我們得先聊聊電信業的⼀個特殊⾓⾊：MVNO （虛擬⾏動網
路電信商）。
MVNO 就是那些⾃⼰沒有實體基地台，靠租⽤  AT&T 、 Verizon 或  T-Mobile 的
網路來賣⾨號的公司，⽐如美國的  Mint Mobile 或  Google Fi 。
對新進者來說，當  MVNO 是最省錢、最快的捷徑。
對任何想進⼊電信市場的玩家⽽⾔，最省錢的劇本都是先跟電信商批發地⾯網
路，在城市和室內使⽤現成的訊號，再搭配⾃⼰的技術補強死⾓。
這樣⼀來，就能⽤極輕的資產，迅速推出⼀張「全美無死⾓」的資費⽅案。

但這個模式能順利推進的前提，是「互補⼤於競爭」。當  SpaceX 只是幫忙補死
⾓時，電信商很樂意賺這筆批發費。
可是，當  SpaceX 公開宣⽰要搶客戶、甚⾄準備⾃⼰買頻譜蓋地⾯網路時，三
⼤巨頭聯⼿警覺：把城市核⼼網路租給⼀個準備⽤低價策略殺價的對⼿，等於
是在割⾃⼰的⾁。批發賺來的錢，根本彌補不了被挖⾛的⽉租費與定價權。
這條原本可能存在的合作路徑，正在迅速變窄。
電信商的防禦動作極其迅速。 10 ⽉  1 ⽇， AT&T 、 T-Mobile 和  Verizon 正式
簽署合約成⽴合資公司，將部分頻譜集中起來，⽤來補⾜傳統基地台打不
到的死⾓。
合資聲明寫得很溫和，但華爾街普遍將此解讀為對  Starlink Mobile 的戰略防
禦。

### Page 21

這場防禦的訊號，在細節中展露無遺。 T-Mobile 悄悄將官網⾸⾴上的「 with
Starlink 」字樣撤下，在⾨⾯上與  SpaceX 劃清界線。
三⼤電信商試圖⾃⼰掌控頻譜主導權，組建⼀個不以  Starlink 為核⼼夥伴的死
⾓平台。
⽽當三⼤電信商組成同盟時，他們需要⼀個衛星通訊的同盟，⽽這個很可
能會是  AST SpaceMobile 。
FOMO 研究院電⼦報  is a reader-supported
publication. To receive new posts and support
my work, consider becoming a free or paid
subscriber.
Subscribed
SpaceX 的破局：買下  800 MHz ⾃⼰建網
既然租電信商城市網路的難度越來越⾼， SpaceX 決定⾃⼰買頻譜。
我們可以把無線頻譜想像成⼀條⾼速公路。⾺路越寬，同⼀時間能塞進去的⾞
⼦通常就越多；可是，如果這條路根本開不到你家⾨⼝，那⾞⼦再多也沒⽤。

通訊頻譜有兩個最關鍵的問題：第⼀是訊號能傳多遠，第⼆是能同時塞多少上
網資料。
( 關於頻譜的詳細分析，可以回看我之前講衛星的深⼊分析，在這裡不再詳述 )
SpaceX 之前透過  EchoStar 拿到了⼀段  2 GHz 的頻譜，這可以當作衛星通訊服
務很重要的「容量」來源。但是，頻率⽐較⾼的訊號有⼀個缺點，遇到牆壁之
類的障礙物⽐較容易被擋住。
⽽這次買下的  800 MHz 頻率⽐較低、波⻑⽐較⻑，不僅傳播條件好，穿透部分
建築物的能⼒也⽐較有優勢。
簡單來說， 2 GHz 負責提供傳輸⼤量資料的空間， 800 MHz 負責鋪設⼀張無死
⾓的覆蓋網。
這讓  SpaceX 拿到了⾃⼰蓋「地⾯網路」的⼊場券，可以把天上的第⼆代衛
星、地⾯的⼩型基地台以及這張低頻訊號網結合起來，打造⼀個完全屬於
⾃⼰的「 Starlink Mobile 」。

### Page 22

有趣的是，這批  800 MHz 頻譜的源頭，其實是  T-Mobile 在  2020 年併購  Sprint
時被政府要求剝離的資產。
T-Mobile ⾃⼰建⽴全國  5G 覆蓋網⽤的是  600 MHz 頻段， 800 MHz 對它⽽⾔只
是重複的冗餘覆蓋。把⼀個⾃⼰根本沒在⽤的資產賣掉，換回  29 億美元現⾦
加上⾃⼰真正需要的  600 MHz 頻譜，這是⼀筆極度理性的資產置換。
後來這批資產轉到了  Grain ⼿中，如今⼜被  SpaceX 以近  80 億美元的⾼價接
⼿。
對  SpaceX ⽽⾔，全國性的低頻頻譜幾乎不可能從三⼤巨頭⼿中流出，這段
800 MHz ，是⾺斯克在美國建⽴「獨⽴⼿機網路」難得能買到的⾨票。
⾃建網的物理現實與時間賽跑
就算拿到了頻譜， SpaceX 接下來⾯對的依然是極度現實的物理限制。
800 MHz 頻譜確實能改善覆蓋範圍，但它無法憑空創造無限的網路容量。
這次買下的頻寬最厚只有  14 MHz ，在某些地區甚⾄只有  5 MHz 。

這是⼀層極薄的覆蓋毯。如果幾⼗萬⼈同時擠在紐約曼哈頓市中⼼看影⽚、開
視訊會議，這種低頻薄頻寬根本撐不住。
城市尖峰時刻的龐⼤資料流量，依然需要⾼頻頻譜與密集的基地台。
因此， SpaceX 的建網策略注定是「衛星為主，微型基站為輔」。
它可能會把⼩型基站直接掛在⽤戶家中的  Starlink 接收盤旁邊，或者只在有⾼
密度需求的地⽅按需部署。這種做法資本⽀出極低，覆蓋範圍會跟著⽤戶慢慢
⻑出來。
這意味著  SpaceX 開台第⼀天，絕對無法提供全國室內無死⾓的完美體驗。
真正的護城河：不在地⾯，在⽕箭
很多⼈盯著  800 MHz 的薄頻寬，覺得這不⾜以撼動三⼤巨頭，卻忽略了
SpaceX 真正的殺⼿鐧：發射成本與星座規模。
SpaceX ⾃⼰造衛星、⾃⼰⽤⽕箭打上去， FCC 最近還批准了多達  15,000 顆下
⼀代  Direct-to-Cell 衛星的部署。

### Page 23

這種「太空基礎設施」的規模優勢，是  AT&T 或  Verizon 花多少錢都買不到
的，也是其他衛星新創難以企及的。
當傳統電信商在地⾯為了幾  MHz 的頻譜⽄⽄計較、為了基地台選址和地⽅政
府博弈時， SpaceX 正在頭頂上佈建⼀張不受地形限制的⽴體網路。
天上⽤下⼀代衛星直連⼿機提供廣覆蓋，地⾯⽤剛買下的  800 MHz 鋪⼀層
穿透⼒強的基礎網。
這套組合拳，才是  SpaceX 敢於直接賣⾨號的底氣。
定價權的倒數計時
根據公司近期的說法，⽐較有意義的  5G 級衛星直連服務，⼤概要到  2028 年上
半年才會開始上線。
即使屆時正式開台，初期體驗也不會完美。室外死⾓會明顯減少，對價格敏
感、不在乎極致網速的⽤戶有吸引⼒；但城市室內與尖峰時段的容量，仍會明
顯落後三⼤電信商。
SpaceX 開台第⼀天賣的，會是⼀個「覆蓋更廣、價格可能更低的基本服務」，
⽽不是⽴刻全⾯取代現有的⾼速體驗。
但是， SpaceX 不需要提供完美的  5G 體驗，只要它提供⼀個「⾜夠便宜且沒有

死⾓」的替代品，就⾜以讓那些對價格敏感的消費者⼼動，進⽽打破電信巨頭
們多年來維持的價格默契。
從投資的⾓度來看，市場重新定價的焦點，早就超越了「明年會流失多少⽤
戶」。
真正被放上天平的，是未來⼗年內，這些依賴⾼資本⽀出築起護城河的⽼字號
公司，是否還能維持過去那種穩定且豐厚的利潤率。
當破壞者拿到了低頻頻譜這張⼊場券，並且擁有對⼿無法複製的太空優勢時，
定價權的流失，往往會⽐⽤戶的流失來得更快。
關於太空，我在年初的時候寫了兩篇，分為衛星和發射的兩篇，有興
趣的朋友可以重溫當中邏輯。

### Page 24

⾺遜， AST SpaceMobile

Document 279: new era of chip testing 20261008 ubs

Source PDF: all report pdf/new era of chip testing 20261008 ubs.pdf
Report source: 未公開來源
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 31
Status: ok

### Page 1

ab 8 October 2026
Global Research
Asia Semiconductor Insight
AI put to the test: the new era of chip testing
Time to revisit semiconductor testing as content growth accelerates
Our core thesis is that AI-driven chip complexity is drastically increasing testing content
across wafer, package, and system levels, creating a multi-year growth opportunity that
the market has yet to fully price in. Testing companies in Taiwan have underperformed
TAIEX in the past three months (indexing at -9% vs TAIEX +7%), yet to recover from the

July market deleveraging. Investor hesitation currently stems from 1) fear of test content
growth expectations already priced-in, 2) lingering concerns over shorter test times, and
3) near-term capacity constraints among major suppliers of probe cards, load boards,
socket pins, testers, and handlers, caused by heightened testing demand. Ultimately,
these concerns overshadow the structural increase in test insertions required by AI
accelerators, chiplets, and advanced packaging. As advanced packaging scales, testing

intensity must rise in tandem, driving growth through higher content per device rather
than semiconductor unit growth alone.
Expanding testing opportunities with shift-left for CP and increasing FT content
Chip probing (CP) supply chain should be the key beneficiary of the industry's shift-left
testing trend, as more test insertions move to the wafer level before packaging, in
addition to longer CP time. We believe investors underestimate this trend, which should

accelerate with broader adoption of 2.5D/3D packaging, chiplets and larger AI
packages, driving demand for high pin count probe cards, probers, and thermal-control
equipment. We expect strong growth for Hermes Testing (HTSI)  as thermal-
management requirements rise with increasing chip power and complexity, with prober
thermal-control module shipments reaching 3,000/4,100 units in 2027E/28E, up from
1,200 in 2026E . We forecast Chunghwa Precision Test (CHPT) 's sales to exceed

consensus by 7%/15% in 2027E/28E, supported by structural AI/HPC testing demand
and rising value-added content. Importantly, shift-left testing does not diminish the role
of final test (FT) and mission-mode testing. Test content and test time should continue to
increase as validation becomes increasingly critical for complex, large-form-factor AI
accelerators. We expect larger package sizes and rising system-level testing
requirements to drive demand for WinWay's high-pin-count sockets, supporting

74%/71% YoY sales growth in 2027E/28E.
Future of testing: co-packaged optics (CPO) will be a major opportunity
CPO testing represents a significant new market opportunity, though we expect the
ecosystem to remain fragmented rather than consolidating around a single winner. As AI
scaling shifts the industry bottleneck from memory to interconnect, CPO adoption is
becoming increasingly important, creating new testing challenges that will require
collaboration across equipment, optics, and test solution providers. Leveraging

ficonTEC's optical alignment expertise and HTSI's probe, testing, and manufacturing
capabilities, we estimate the HTSI  will deliver 40 units of insertion 2 equipment and 70
units of insertion 3 testing equipment in 2027 , with further upside in 2028 as CPO
deployment expands from scale-out towards scale-up.
Initiate Hermes Testing, Chunghwa Precision Test, WinWay with Buy ratings
We initiate coverage on semiconductor testing solution provider Hermes Testing with

a Buy rating,  NT$8,880 price target based on 31x 2028E PE, for thermal control/CPO
with the most attractive risk/reward in our testing coverage; test interface PCB supplier
CHPT with a Buy rating, NT$5,000 price target based on 31x avg 2027-28E PE, for
CPU/TPU programs and test board content gain; and semiconductor test socket leader
WinWay with a Buy rating, NT$8,900 price target based on 30x avg 2027-28E PE,
for socket content growth and margin recovery. Among our testing coverage, we also

including information on the Quantitative Research Review published by UBS, begin on page 22.

Q: Is chip probing becoming increasingly important in the testing supply chain?
Yes.   As package costs rise, foundries must improve known-good-die (KGD) screening before
packaging, increasing demand for wafer-level testing. This should drive demand for probe cards,

probers and advanced thermal control solutions capable of managing higher power consumption,
wafer warpage and tighter test tolerances. We forecast HTSI/CHPT/WinWay' probe card business to
see a 31%/35%/33% sales CAGR for 2026-2031E, as they are positioned to grow strongly given
their smaller scale and robust growth drivers from shift left, CPU, AI accelerators. We forecast HTSI to
ship 1,200/3,000/4,100 thermal control modules in 2026E/27E/28E, as advanced wafer-level test for

AI accelerators will require thermal control solutions in order to prevent probe card needle burnt.
Q: Will FT and SLT content continue to grow in the coming years?
Yes. Despite concerns that final-test (FT) time could flatten or decline with Nvidia's Rubin GPUs, we
expect overall FT content to continue growing as advanced packaging complexity and chip sizes
increase. We also expect broader adoption of system-level testing (SLT)/mission-mode testing, as AI

accelerators require increasingly stringent validation to ensure optimal system and rack-level
performance. We forecast WinWay's socket business to see a 33% sales CAGR for 2026-2031E.
Q: Will CPO testing be a revenue generator in 2027-28 to supply chain players?
Yes. Testing remains a key bottleneck, creating a new equipment opportunity beginning in 2027E.
We expect initial CPO volumes in 2027, followed by broader adoption in 2028. Testing remains one

of the key bottlenecks to commercialization, driving demand for new equipment and test solutions.
We believe early leaders such as ficonTEC (partnership with HTSI) are well positioned to benefit from
the initial ramp, alongside suppliers including Chroma ATE and Hon Precision.
1) We believe upside from agentic AI CPU demand is not reflected in CHPT and WinWay shares. We
estimate CPU-related demand could contribute 20-30% of 2027E sales  for CHPT and WinWay,

respectively, with further upside in 2028E. 2) We believe market expectations for 2027 CPO testing
equipment shipments remain conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on our
industry analysis, we estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, this
should be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3 shipment
due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO adoption expands.

Testing is becoming one of the largest incremental beneficiaries of AI-driven semiconductor
complexity. As more test insertions are required across wafer, package and system levels, testing
content per device should increase materially, expanding the industry's addressable market and
supporting multi-year growth across the supply chain.
TSMC and OSATs have been expanding their CoWoS capacities aggressively, from 40kwpm in 2024
to around 90 kwpm in 2025, and tracking to expand CoWoS capacity toward 160kwpm  in 2026E.

TSMC also ramped up expanding N3/N2 capacity, with multiple product launches from customers'
pipelines—Nvidia's Rubin, AMD's MI400 series/Venice CPU and other cloud ASICs—translating into
foreseeable, strong testing demand.
